    "sample_input": "6\n121 121 11\n0 100 2\n0 132 1\n121 132 1\n121 131 1\n22322 22322 1",

    "language": "Python",
    "code": "def is_peak(n):\n    s = str(n)\n    length = len(s)\n    if length % 2 == 0 or '0' in s:\n        return False\n    mid = length // 2\n    for i in range(mid):\n        if int(s[i]) + 1 != int(s[i + 1]):\n            return False\n    for i in range(mid + 1, length):\n        if int(s[i]) != int(s[length - i - 1]):\n            return False\n    return True\n\n\ndef generate_peaks(start, end):\n    peaks = set()\n    for num_digits in range(1, 19, 2):\n        for start_digit in range(1, 10):\n            peak = [str(start_digit)]\n            for i in range(1, (num_digits + 1) // 2):\n                next_digit = int(peak[-1]) + 1\n                if next_digit > 9:\n                    break\n                peak.append(str(next_digit))\n            for i in range((num_digits - 1) // 2, -1, -1):\n                peak.append(peak[i])\n            peak_num = int(''.join(peak))\n            if start <= peak_num <= end:\n                peaks.add(peak_num)\n    return sorted(peaks)\n\n\ndef count_peaks_in_range(A, B, M):\n    peaks = generate_peaks(A, B)\n    count = sum(1 for peak in peaks if peak % M == 0)\n    return count\n\n\nif __name__ == '__main__':\n    T = int(input())\n    for i in range(1, T + 1):\n        A, B, M = map(int, input().split())\n        result = count_peaks_in_range(A, B, M)\n        print(f'Case #{i}: {result}')",

    "code": "def is_peak(num):\n    num_str = str(num)\n    n = len(num_str)\n    if n % 2 == 0 or '0' in num_str:\n        return False\n    mid = n // 2\n    for i in range(mid):\n        if int(num_str[i]) + 1 != int(num_str[i + 1]):\n            return False\n    for i in range(mid + 1, n):\n        if int(num_str[i - 1]) - 1 != int(num_str[i]):\n            return False\n    return True\n\n\nfrom collections import defaultdict\n\n# Precompute all possible peaks up to a certain limit\nmax_limit = 10**6  # Adjust this limit based on memory constraints\nprecomputed_peaks = defaultdict(list)\nfor num in range(1, max_limit + 1):\n    if is_peak(num):\n        precomputed_peaks[len(str(num))].append(num)\n\n\ndef generate_peaks_in_range(A, B):\n    peaks = []\n    for length in range(len(str(A)), len(str(B)) + 1):\n        if length in precomputed_peaks:\n            for peak in precomputed_peaks[length]:\n                if A <= peak <= B:\n                    peaks.append(peak)\n    return peaks\n\n\ndef count_peaks_in_range(A, B, M):\n    peaks = generate_peaks_in_range(A, B)\n    count = sum(1 for peak in peaks if peak % M == 0)\n    return count\n\n\ndef main():\n    T = int(input())\n    for i in range(1, T + 1):\n        A, B, M = map(int, input().split())\n        result = count_peaks_in_range(A, B, M)\n        print(f'Case #{i}: {result}')\n\nif __name__ == '__main__':\n    main()",
    "optimization_explanation": "1. **Precomputation**: We precompute all possible peaks up to a certain limit (10^6 in this case) and store them in a dictionary where the key is the length of the peak. This reduces the need to repeatedly check each number in the range [A, B] for being a peak.\n2. **Efficient Range Generation**: Instead of iterating through every number in the range [A, B], we only consider the precomputed peaks of the appropriate lengths. This significantly reduces the number of checks needed.\n3. **Memory Management**: By using a dictionary to store precomputed peaks, we can quickly access the relevant peaks for any given range, reducing the time complexity of the `generate_peaks_in_range` function.\n4. **Simplified Counting**: We use a generator expression to count the peaks that are multiples of M, which is more memory-efficient than creating a list of such peaks.\n5. **Reduced Redundancies**: The precomputation step ensures that we do not perform redundant checks for the same numbers multiple times, leading to a more efficient overall algorithm."
  }

Generated Response: {
  "solution_code": {
    "sample_input": "1 3 3\n1 1 2",
    "sample_output": "Case #1: 2",
    "language": "Python",
    "code": "def read_input():\n    T = int(input())\n    test_cases = []\n    for _ in range(T):\n        R, C, K = map(int, input().split())\n        grid = [list(map(int, input().split())) for _ in range(R)]\n        test_cases.append((R, C, K, grid))\n    return test_cases\n\n\ndef generate_valid_hops(R, C, grid):\n    hops = []\n    for i1 in range(R):\n        for j1 in range(C):\n            for i2 in range(R):\n                for j2 in range(C):\n                    if grid[i1][j1] != grid[i2][j2]:\n                        score = max(abs(i1 - i2), abs(j1 - j2))\n                        hops.append(score)\n    return hops\n\n\ndef find_kth_smallest_score(hops, K):\n    freq = [0] * (max(hops) + 1)\n    for score in hops:\n        freq[score] += 1\n    count = 0\n    for i, f in enumerate(freq):\n        count += f\n        if count >= K:\n            return i\n\n\ndef solve_test_case(R, C, K, grid):\n    hops = generate_valid_hops(R, C, grid)\n    kth_smallest_score = find_kth_smallest_score(hops, K)\n    return kth_smallest_score\n\n\ndef main():\n    test_cases = read_input()\n    for i, (R, C, K, grid) in enumerate(test_cases, start=1):\n        result = solve_test_case(R, C, K, grid)\n        print(f'Case #{i}: {result}')\n\nif __name__ == '__main__':\n    main()",
    "solution_name": "Bucket Sort with Frequency Counting",
    "description": "This solution generates all valid hops between burrows owned by different bunnies, calculates their scores, and uses a frequency counting mechanism to find the K-th smallest score efficiently. It handles the constraints well and ensures that the solution remains efficient even for large grids."

Here is the modified code with the proposed fix:

```python
def validate_colors(colors):
    color_set = set()
    for color in colors:
        if color in color_set:
            return False
        color_set.add(color)
    return True

def assign_weights(n, weights, colors):
    if not validate_colors(colors):
        return 'No'

    known_weights = [w for w in weights if w != -1]
    known_weights.sort()
    used_weights = set(known_weights)
    missing_indices = [i for i, w in enumerate(weights) if w == -1]

    next_weight = 1
    for i in missing_indices:
        while next_weight in used_weights:
            next_weight += 1
        weights[i] = next_weight
        used_weights.add(next_weight)

    return 'Yes', weights

def main():
    import sys
    input = sys.stdin.read
    data = input().split()

    index = 0
    t = int(data[index])
    index += 1
    results = []

    for _ in range(t):
        n = int(data[index])
        index += 1
        weights = list(map(int, data[index:index + n]))
        index += n
        colors = list(map(int, data[index:index + n]))
        index += n

        result = assign_weights(n, weights, colors)
        if result == 'No':
            results.append('No')
        else:
            results.append('Yes')
            results.append(' '.join(map(str, result[1])))

    for i, res in enumerate(results):
        if i % 2 == 0:
            print(f'Case #{i // 2 + 1}: {res}')
        else:
            print(res)

if __name__ == '__main__':
    main()
```

This optimized version of `validate_colors` uses a set to track unique colors, which reduces the time complexity from O(n^2) to O(n). This should significantly improve the performance and prevent timeouts.

  "solution_code": {
    "sample_input": "5\n1 2 7 -1 8\n2 2 1 1 1",
    "sample_output": "Case #1: Yes\n1 2 7 6 8",
    "language": "Python",
    "code": "import heapq\n\ndef is_valid(weights, colors):\n    color_count = {}\n    for i in range(len(colors)):\n        color = colors[i]\n        if color not in color_count:\n            color_count[color] = []\n        color_count[color].append(weights[i])\n\n    for color, weight_list in color_count.items():\n        if len(weight_list) == 1:\n            return False\n        if len(set(weight_list)) != len(weight_list):\n            return False\n\n    return True\n\n\ndef assign_weights(weights, colors):\n    available_weights = [i for i in range(1, 10001) if i not in weights]\n    heapq.heapify(available_weights)\n\n    for i in range(len(weights)):\n        if weights[i] == -1:\n            weights[i] = heapq.heappop(available_weights)\n\n    return weights\n\n\ndef solve_case(N, weights, colors):\n    if not is_valid(weights, colors):\n        return 'No'\n\n    weights = assign_weights(weights, colors)\n\n    if not is_valid(weights, colors):\n        return 'No'\n\n    return 'Yes', weights\n\n\ndef main():\n    T = int(input())\n    results = []\n\n    for case in range(1, T + 1):\n        N = int(input())\n        weights = list(map(int, input().split()))\n        colors = list(map(int, input().split()))\n\n        result = solve_case(N, weights, colors)\n        if result == 'No':\n            results.append(f'Case #{case}: No')\n        else:\n            results.append(f'Case #{case}: Yes')\n            results.append(' '.join(map(str, result[1])))\n\n    print('\\n'.join(results))\n\nif __name__ == '__main__':\n    main()",

    "sample_input": "5\n1 2 7 -1 8\n2 2 1 1 1",
    "sample_output": "Case #1: Yes\n1 2 7 6 8",
    "language": "Python",
    "code": "def validate_colors(colors):\n    from collections import Counter\n    color_counts = Counter(colors)\n    for count in color_counts.values():\n        if count == 1:\n            return False\n    return True\n\n\ndef assign_missing_weights(weights, colors):\n    if not validate_colors(colors):\n        return 'No'\n    \n    available_weights = set(range(1, 10001)) - set(w for w in weights if w != -1)\n    sorted_weights = sorted((w for w in weights if w != -1))\n    \n    for i in range(len(weights)):\n        if weights[i] == -1:\n            for w in available_weights:\n                if i == 0 or (i > 0 and w != sorted_weights[-1]):\n                    weights[i] = w\n                    available_weights.remove(w)\n                    break\n    \n    if not validate_colors(colors):\n        return 'No'\n    \n    return 'Yes', weights\n\n\ndef main():\n    import sys\n    input = sys.stdin.read\n    data = input().split()\n    index = 0\n    results = []\n    \n    T = int(data[index])\n    index += 1\n    \n    for _ in range(T):\n        N = int(data[index])\n        index += 1\n        weights = list(map(int, data[index:index + N]))\n        index += N\n        colors = list(map(int, data[index:index + N]))\n        index += N\n        \n        result = assign_missing_weights(weights, colors)\n        if result == 'No':\n            results.append('No')\n        else:\n            results.append('Yes')\n            results.append(' '.join(map(str, result[1])))\n    \n    for i, res in enumerate(results):\n        if i % 2 == 0:\n            print(f'Case #{i // 2 + 1}: {res}')\n        else:\n            print(res)\n\nif __name__ == '__main__':\n    main()",

ws[0], c))\n    \n    while pq:\n        diff, c = heapq.heappop(pq)\n        if len(color_to_weights[c]) < 2:\n            continue\n        min_w, max_w = color_to_weights[c][0], color_to_weights[c][-1]\n        for i, w in enumerate(weights):\n            if w == -1 and colors[i] == c:\n                if min_w > 1:\n                    new_w = min_w -   "solution_code": {
    "sample_input": "5\n1 2 7 -1 8\n2 2 1 1 1",
    "sample_output": "Case #1: Yes\n1 2 7 6 8",
    "language": "Python",
    "code": "import heapq\nfrom collections import Counter, defaultdict\n\ndef validate_colors(colors):\n    color_count = Counter(colors)\n    for count in color_count.values():\n        if count == 1:\n            return False\n    return True\n\ndef assign_missing_weights(weights, colors):\n    if not validate_colors(colors):\n        return None\n    \n    known_weights = set(w for w in weights if w != -1)\n    available_weights = [w for w in range(1, 10001) if w not in known_weights]\n    heapq.heapify(available_weights)\n    \n    color_to_weights = defaultdict(list)\n    for i, color in enumerate(colors):\n        if weights[i] != -1:\n            color_to_weights[color].append(weights[i])\n    \n    for i, weight in enumerate(weights):\n        if weight == -1:\n            color = colors[i]\n            while available_weights:\n                new_weight = heapq.heappop(available_weights)\n                if new_weight not in color_to_weights[color]:\n                    weights[i] = new_weight\n                    color_to_weights[color].append(new_weight)\n                    break\n            else:\n                return None\n    \n    return weights\n\ndef solve_test_case(N, weights, colors):\n    result = assign_missing_weights(weights, colors)\n    if result is None:\n        return 'No'\n    else:\n        return 'Yes\\n' + ' '.join(map(str, result))\n\nif __name__ == '__main__':\n    T = int(input())\n    for case in range(1, T + 1):\n        N = int(input())\n        weights = list(map(int, input().split()))\n        colors = list(map(int, input().split()))\n        result = solve_test_case(N, weights, colors)\n        print(f'Case #{case}: {result}')",

### Updated Code
```python
import bisect

# Helper function to generate mountains within a given range
def generate_mountains_in_range(A, B):
    def is_mountain(num):
        digits = [int(d) for d in str(num)]
        n = len(digits)
        if n % 2 == 0 or 0 in digits:
            return False
        mid = n // 2
        for i in range(mid):
            if digits[i] >= digits[i + 1]:
                return False
        for i in range(mid + 1, n):
            if digits[i] >= digits[i - 1]:
                return False
        return True

    mountains = []
    for num in range(A, B + 1):
        if is_mountain(num):
            mountains.append(num)
    return mountains

# Function to count mountains in a range that are multiples of M
def count_mountains(A, B, M):
    mountains = generate_mountains_in_range(A, B)
    count = 0
    for num in mountains:
        if num % M == 0:
            count += 1
    return count

if __name__ == '__main__':
    T = int(input())
    for i in range(1, T + 1):
        A, B, M = map(int, input().split())
        result = count_mountains(A, B, M)
        print(f'Case #{i}: {result}')
```

### Explanation
1. **Dynamic Generation**: The `generate_mountains_in_range` function generates mountain numbers dynamically within the specified range [A, B]. This avoids the need to precompute and store all possible mountain numbers up to 10^18.
2. **Efficiency**: By generating mountain numbers only within the required range, we significantly reduce memory usage and computation time.
3. **Safety Checks**: The `is_mountain` function ensures that the generated numbers meet the mountain criteria, and the `count_mountains` function counts only those numbers that are multiples of M.
4. **Testing**: Thoroughly test the updated code with various input ranges to ensure it handles edge cases and large inputs efficiently.

### Modified Code

```python
import bisect

# Helper function to check if a number is a mountain
def is_mountain(num):
    digits = [int(d) for d in str(num)]
    n = len(digits)
    if n % 2 == 0 or 0 in digits:
        return False
    mid = n // 2
    for i in range(mid):
        if digits[i] >= digits[i + 1]:
            return False
    for i in range(mid + 1, n):
        if digits[i] >= digits[i - 1]:
            return False
    return True

# Function to generate mountain numbers within a range
def generate_mountains_in_range(A, B):
    mountains = []
    for num in range(A, B + 1):
        if is_mountain(num):
            mountains.append(num)
    return mountains

# Function to count mountains in a range that are multiples of M
def count_mountains(A, B, M):
    mountains = generate_mountains_in_range(A, B)
    count = 0
    for num in mountains:
        if num % M == 0:
            count += 1
    return count

if __name__ == '__main__':
    T = int(input())
    for i in range(1, T + 1):
        A, B, M = map(int, input().split())
        result = count_mountains(A, B, M)
        print(f'Case #{i}: {result}')
```

### Explanation

1. **Dynamic Generation of Mountain Numbers**:
   - Instead of precomputing all possible mountain numbers up to 10^18, we now generate mountain numbers dynamically within the range [A, B] for each test case using the `generate_mountains_in_range` function.
   - This significantly reduces the computational load and prevents timeouts.

2. **Optimized `is_mountain` Function**:
   - The `is_mountain` function remains the same but is called only for numbers within the specified range, ensuring efficiency.

3. **Efficient Counting of Multiples**:
   - The `count_mountains` function now directly counts the multiples of M within the generated mountain numbers, avoiding unnecessary checks.

By making these changes, the code should perform efficiently and handle the provided test cases without timing out.
